Backtests/n8n/run-20261123-070205/#30871

Issue #30871 Merged fix PR #36716 Run 23 Nov 2026 · run-20261123-070205 n8n-io/n8n

[Bug] silent imap death

Better

Our patch was judged better than the merged fix.

In this judgment, Patch B = our agent, Patch A = the merged fix.

Both patches target the same failure mode: node-imap can emit `error` without a following `close`, and the existing `onTransportError` only called `lose()` and returned when a restore was possible, waiting on a close that never comes. That leaves exactly the symptom the issue describes: no 'closed unexpectedly' log (it is tied to `close`), no reconnect, no error handler, and a SELECT that hangs forever if the error lands mid-dial.

Patch B fixes that root cause directly and narrowly: `onTransportError` now starts `restore()` itself (and reports when restoring is impossible), the dialling transport's error is let go through the SELECT rather than tearing down the installed one, and `selectMailbox` rejects on `error` so a reconnect attempt cannot hang. Its tests exercise precisely the issue scenario (error with no close → reconnect; tries exhausted → `close('dropped')` reaches the caller; fresh transport erroring under SELECT is released) and would fail on the old code. Changes are ~40 lines in one file with no behaviour change for users.

Patch A replaces the IMAP library wholesale (node-imap → imapflow), adds a vendored patch to imapflow, rewrites header parsing, attachment handling, part selection, and introduces a new search-criteria translator that now throws on activation for inputs previously accepted (empty criteria, `KEYWORD \Recent`, colliding keys handled via De Morgan rewrites), changes `addFlags`' signature, drops rfc2047 filename decoding in favour of driver metadata, and rewrites essentially every test in the package. Its genuine strength is liveness detection via `maxIdleTime`/`socketTimeout`, verified against a fake server that goes silent, which would also catch a truly wedged socket. But that advantage is bought with a multi-thousand-line redesign whose correctness cannot be established from the diff, that buries the actual fix, and that carries real regression risk for existing workflows (stricter criteria compilation, output/attachment naming differences, a patched dependency). Under the stated weighting, that extra scope counts against A because it adds risk and obscures the fix.

The independent tests crashed on all three trees, so they provide no signal. On the evidence in front of me, B resolves the identified root cause with targeted, meaningful tests and minimal risk; A's broader detection does not outweigh the risk and opacity of a full library migration as the fix for this bug.

Four judges ruled on this ticket in parallel, and the verdict comes from counting their votes. Each saw the issue and the two patches, labelled A and B in random order (two of them saw ours as A), with issue links and version numbers removed. Where tests could check the fix, they also saw the results of tests a separate agent wrote for the issue, run on both patches. None was told which patch was ours. The reasoning above is one judge's, shown verbatim.

The issue

View on GitHub

Bug Description

The Email Trigger (IMAP) node can silently lose its connection without producing any log output, without triggering a reconnection attempt, and without invoking the configured Error Handler workflow. The workflow continues to show as "Published" (active) in the UI, but the IMAP trigger is effectively dead. Incoming emails remain unprocessed indefinitely. The user has no way to detect this condition other than manually noticing unread emails.

Additional context

This was observed after a Hetzner Cloud Firewall rule change that temporarily interrupted connectivity on port 993. Under normal circumstances, IMAP disconnects are logged and trigger reconnection attempts. In this case, the connection was lost during or after the firewall change, and the trigger node entered a state where it believed it was still connected but had no active TCP socket.
The forceReconnect option (set to 55 minutes) did not recover the connection either, suggesting the internal reconnect timer may also have died silently.
Related: #29996 (Options-only changes not restarting triggers on Publish)

To Reproduce

The exact trigger for the silent failure is not yet identified, but the following sequence was observed:

  1. Workflow "Parse DMARC reports…" is active with an Email Trigger (IMAP) node (typeVersion 2) and forceReconnect: 55 configured
  2. The IMAP trigger is connected and processing emails normally
  3. An external event disrupts the IMAP connection (in this case: a Hetzner Cloud Firewall rule for port 993 was temporarily removed and re-added)
  4. The IMAP trigger loses its connection
  5. No Email Read Imap: Connected closed unexpectedly log message is produced
  6. No reconnection attempt is logged
  7. No Error Handler workflow is triggered
  8. The workflow continues to show "Published" in the UI
  9. ss -tn | grep 993 inside the container confirms: no active TCP connection to the IMAP server
  10. Emails accumulate unread in the mailbox
  11. Only a full container restart (docker restart) re-establishes the IMAP connection

Expected behavior

When an IMAP connection is lost, the trigger node should:

  1. Log the disconnection event (as it normally does with "Connected closed unexpectedly")
  2. Attempt to reconnect (with exponential backoff, as it normally does)
  3. If reconnection fails permanently, invoke the configured Error Handler workflow
  4. The workflow status in the UI should reflect the actual state of the trigger

A silently dead trigger with no logging, no reconnection, and no error notification is the worst possible failure mode — the user has no indication that mail processing has stopped.
